<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
  <head>
    <meta name="author" content="Janosh Riebesell" />
    <!-- FOUC mirror of svelte-widgets apply_theme_mode. -->
    <script>
      {
        let effective_theme
        try {
          effective_theme = localStorage.getItem(`theme`)
        } catch {}
        if (effective_theme !== `light` && effective_theme !== `dark`)
          effective_theme = globalThis.matchMedia?.(`(prefers-color-scheme: dark)`)
            ?.matches
            ? `dark`
            : `light`
        document.documentElement.style.colorScheme = effective_theme
        document.documentElement.dataset.theme = effective_theme
      }
    </script>
    <meta
      name="keywords"
      content="materials discovery, inorganic crystal stability, machine learning, interatomic potential, Bayesian optimization, high-throughput search, convex hull, formation energy"
    />

    <meta name="twitter:site" content="@jrib_" />

RSS</a></div> <figcaption class="caption-radar-container svelte-1uha8ag"><div style="background-color: var(--card-bg); padding: 0.2em 0.5em; border-radius: 4px">The <strong>CPS</strong> (Combined Performance Score) is a metric that weights
      discovery performance (F1), geometry optimization quality (RMSD), and thermal
      conductivity prediction accuracy (κ<sub>SRME</sub>). Use the radar chart to adjust
      the importance of each component. <br/><br/> The training set column shows the number of materials used to train the model. For models
      trained on DFT relaxations, we show the number of distinct frames in parentheses. In cases
      where only the number of frames is known, we report the number of frames as the training
      set size. <code>(N=x)</code> in the Model Params column shows the number of
      estimators if an ensemble was used. <!--[0-->DAF = Discovery Acceleration Factor measures how many more stable materials a
        model finds compared to random selection from the test set. The unique structure
        prototypes in the WBM test set <!--[0-->have a <code>15.3%</code> rate of stable crystals, meaning the max possible DAF is <code>(32.9k / 215k)^−1
            ≈ 6.54</code>.<!--]--><!--]--></div> <div class="radar-chart svelte-13vqecb" style="--chart-size: 260px"><div class="chart-title svelte-13vqecb"><span class="metric-name svelte-13vqecb" title="Combined Performance Score averages discovery (F1), structure optimization (RMSD), and phonon performance (κ&lt;sub>SRME&lt;/sub>) according to user-defined weights.   cohort as the metrics table above, following the active task preset and table filters.</p> <div class="bleed-1400 collapsible-legend svelte-hzeknc" style="margin-block: 2em"><div class="controls-row svelte-hzeknc"><label for="size-select" class="svelte-hzeknc">Marker Size</label> <!--$s6--><div class="multiselect svelte-kopoqf single" data-id="size-select" tabindex="-1" style="flex: 1; height: 600px" class="scatter plot-frame svelte-6qmnux"><!--[-1--><!--]--> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <!----></div><!----></div><!----> <!--[--><!--]--> <h4 align="center" class="toc-exclude" style="display: none;"><p><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.14920" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/arXiv-2308.14920-blue?logo=arxiv&amp;logoColor=white" alt="arXiv"/></a> <a href="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/actions/workflows/test.yml" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/actions/workflows/test.yml/badge.svg" alt="Tests"/></a> <a href="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/actions/workflows/gh-pages.yml" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/actions/workflows/gh-pages.yml/badge.svg" alt="GitHub Pages"/></a> <a href="https://python.org/downloads" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://img.shields.io/badge/Python-3.14+-blue.svg?logo=python&amp;logoColor=white" alt="Requires Python 3.14+"/></a> <a href="https://pypi.org/project/matbench-discovery?logo=pypi&amp;logoColor=white" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/matbench-discovery?logo=pypi&amp;logoColor=white" alt="PyPI"/></a></p></h4> <p>Matbench Discovery is an <a href="https://janosh.github.io/matbench-discovery" rel="nofollow">interactive leaderboard</a> that ranks ML interatomic potentials across crystal stability prediction, geometry optimization, phonons and thermal conductivity, molecular dynamics, and diatomic potential-energy curves.</p> <p>We rank <!--[--><!---->42<!--]--> models covering multiple methodologies including graph neural network (GNN) interatomic potentials, GNN one-shot predictors, iterative Bayesian optimizers and random forests with shallow-learning structure fingerprints.</p> <!--[--><!--[0--><span id="best-report"><a href="/models/equiformer-v3-oam">EquiformerV3+DeNS-OAM</a> leads the
        Discovery view with the best <!---->F1<!----> of 0.931
        .</span><!--]--><!--]--> <p>The benchmark exposes accuracy, robustness, and computational-cost trade-offs across these tasks to help users choose models for static and finite-temperature materials simulations.</p> <blockquote><p>📖 <strong>Important:</strong> In Matbench Discovery, the convex hull used to evaluate stability is constructed from DFT reference energies, not from model predictions. This differs from some other benchmarking approaches and has important implications for metric interpretation. See <a href="https://janosh.github.io/matbench-discovery/tasks/discovery#convex-hull-construction-in-matbench-discovery" rel="nofollow"><code>/tasks/discovery</code></a> for more information.</p></blockquote> <p>To cite Matbench Discovery, use:</p> <blockquote><p>Riebesell, J., Goodall, R.E.A., Benner, P. et al. A framework to evaluate machine learning crystal stability predictions. Nat Mach Intell 7, 836–847 (2025). <a href="https://doi.org/10.1038/s42256-025-01055-1" rel="nofollow">https://doi.org/10.1038/s42256-025-01055-1</a></p></blockquote> <p>Are you submitting a new model? Follow the <a href="https://janosh.github.io/matbench-discovery/contribute" rel="nofollow">contributing guide</a>, including its branch-specific PR instructions for loading the model checklist. For other changes, use GitHub’s documented <a href="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/compare" rel="nofollow">branch selector</a> to open a regular PR with a blank description. Ask support questions via <a href="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/discussions" rel="nofollow">GitHub discussion</a>.</p> <blockquote><p>Disclaimer: We evaluate how accurately ML models predict several material properties like thermodynamic stability, thermal conductivity, and atomic positions, in all cases using PBE DFT as reference data. Although these properties are important for high-throughput materials discovery, the ranking cannot give a complete picture of a model’s overall ability to drive materials research. A high ranking does not constitute endorsement by the Materials Project.</p></blockquote><!----> <blockquote>🆕 <strong>New task — Molecular Dynamics.</strong> Matbench Discovery now scores how
  faithfully MLIPs reproduce finite-temperature observables of ab-initio MD (AIMD): radial
  distribution functions, vibrational density of states, pressure distributions, and
  single-point energy/force RMSEs. Explore the new metrics on the <a href="/tasks/md">MD leaderboard</a>.</blockquote> <blockquote><p>⚠️ <strong>Interpret with caution.</strong> The molecular dynamics task is preliminary. The DynaMat v1.0 reference test set and the metrics are still evolving. Treat the current MD metrics and ranking as indicative only, expect changes as test set and metrics evolve.</p> <p>The reference set currently holds 17 structures (DynaMat v1.0, spanning pure metals, alloys, high-entropy alloys, transition-metal dichalcogenides, perovskites and molecular crystals at 293–1500 K); an upcoming <strong>v2 release will grow this AIMD test set to ~100 structures</strong>. Collabs to grow it even further welcome! The public reference data intentionally omits energies and forces. Energy/force RMSEs shown here are maintainer-computed private-label diagnostics and are excluded from CMDS, which ranks trajectory-level observables plus speed. All models currently on the leaderboard were run through a unified script, <a href="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/blob/main/models/run_md.py" rel="nofollow"><code>models/run_md.py</code></a>. If your model isn’t listed, we invite you to run it and submit your metrics via <a href="https://github.com/janosh/matbench-discovery/blob/HEAD/contributing.md" rel="nofollow">PR</a>.</p> <p>For details on the MD modeling task, the DynaMat reference set and the CMDS metric, refer to <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03433" rel="nofollow">arXiv:2607.03433</a>.</p></blockquote><!----> <h2 id="github-activity">GitHub Activity</h2> <p>Development activity and community engagement of MLIP GitHub repos. Points are sized by
  number of contributors and colored by number of commits over the last year.</p> <div class="bleed-1400" style="margin-block: 2em"><div style="--plot-frame-width: var(--scatter-width, 100%);
